### User

Recheck and Improve the System
Please recheck the entire system thoroughly and provide the findings as a `.md` file.
The objective is to determine whether the system can be improved further in any way. Do not limit the analysis to the current approach. Look for potential improvements using any suitable method, including:

* Mathematical models
* Financial models
* Statistical methods
* Algorithms
* Optimization techniques
* LLM/AI layers
* Architecture changes
* Better decision-making approaches
* Any other relevant technology or methodology

Compare the existing approach with possible alternatives and identify where a different method could provide better results, accuracy, efficiency, reliability, scalability, or cost-effectiveness.
Also check whether previous audit findings, assumptions, or recommendations are still valid and whether anything important has been missed.
Be critical and do not assume the current system is already optimal.
The final `.md` report should clearly explain:

1. What is already good.
2. What can be improved.
3. What new approaches could be considered.
4. Why each proposed improvement may be better.
5. Any risks, trade-offs, or additional complexity.
6. Which improvements are worth implementing and which are not.

Focus especially on finding new, meaningful improvements that may have been overlooked previously, rather than simply repeating the existing audit findings.

### DeepSeek AI

Sections 4–6 lay out prioritised improvements with impact/effort/cost. Section 7 gives a phased rollout plan.</span></p><hr><h2><span class="">1. What Is Already Good (Preserve Vigorously)</span></h2><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">These decisions are correct and should </span><strong><span class="">not</span></strong><span class=""> be touched without strong cause:</span></p><ol start="1"><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Deterministic Risk Arbiter primacy.</span></strong><span class=""> LLMs are advisory. All sizing/SL/targets come from </span>`src/risk/arbiter.py`<span class="">. This is the single most important architectural decision in the codebase and protects against every class of prompt-injection / hallucination risk.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Fail-closed semantics.</span></strong><span class=""> </span>`is_system_halted()`<span class=""> returns </span>`True`<span class=""> on exception; Shariah gate fails on missing data; anti-trap layers fail on null trigger. This is correct defensive coding.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Circuit-lock shield.</span></strong><span class=""> Detecting </span>`ltp &lt;= lower_circuit`<span class=""> and marking </span>`MARKED_FOR_CLOSURE`<span class=""> instead of pretending a stop-out fill happened at the locked price is </span><em><span class="">the</span></em><span class=""> correct behaviour for Indian markets. Most retail systems would simulate a fake fill and produce a corrupted equity curve.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Two-phase exit (T1=50% trim + breakeven ratchet, T2=runner).</span></strong><span class=""> This is textbook Minervini/O'Neil. The 0.5% friction buffer on the breakeven stop is a thoughtful detail.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Corporate action retro-adjustment applied to </span><em><span class="">both</span></em><span class=""> </span>`bhavcopy_daily`<span class=""> and open </span>`positions`<span class="">.</span></strong><span class=""> Missing this destroys risk logic on the day of a split.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Single-writer DuckDB with </span>`portalocker`<span class=""> + thread-local re-entrancy.</span></strong><span class=""> Proper solution for embedded OLAP in a multi-cron environment.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Kill-switch persisted in DB, checked at pipeline entry.</span></strong><span class=""> Freeze state survives process restarts.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Paper trading gate enforced by </span>`PAPER_TRADING_MODE`<span class=""> sync with </span>`EXECUTION_ENV`<span class="">.</span></strong><span class=""> Prevents accidental live deployment.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Waterfall LLM with deterministic fallback.</span></strong><span class=""> Zero-token-cost failure mode is preserved.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade card HTML parsing (</span>`parse_mode=HTML`<span class=""> with </span>`html.escape`<span class="">).</span></strong><span class=""> Prevents 400 crashes from LLM punctuation — a subtle, real-world bug that many systems ship with.</span></p></li></ol><hr><h2><span class="">2. What Can Be Improved (Detailed Findings)</span></h2><h3><span class="">F1 🔴 No Portfolio-Level Correlation / Cluster Control</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Current state:</span></strong><span class=""> </span>`arbiter.py`<span class=""> checks sector count and sector capital %, but two micro-cap pharma stocks can move 90% together on a single regulatory headline. A trader can end up with 4 "different" positions that are effectively one leveraged bet.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why it matters:</span></strong><span class=""> The 6% monthly drawdown circuit-breaker assumes diversification. Without correlation control, a single sector shock can hit it in one session.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Concrete fix (low complexity, high impact):</span></strong></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Add a </span>`correlation_guard.py`<span class=""> module that, before each new entry, computes the 60-day Pearson correlation of log returns between the candidate and every open position.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Reject if </span><strong><span class="">average pairwise correlation &gt; 0.65</span></strong><span class=""> OR </span><strong><span class="">max correlation &gt; 0.80</span></strong><span class="">.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Additionally, compute the </span><strong><span class="">effective number of independent bets</span></strong><span class=""> (ENB) using the Marchenko–Pastur eigenvalue spectrum of the position return covariance matrix. Refuse new trades if </span>`ENB &lt; 2.5`<span class=""> (i.e., current book behaves like &lt;2.5 independent bets).</span></p></li></ul><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better than alternatives:</span></strong><span class=""> Cheaper than full HRP optimization, deterministic (no ML), and prevents the worst-case concentration. Fits the existing "fail-closed" design.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> May reject some winning setups in trending sectors. Mitigate with a </span>`correlation_override_after_days`<span class=""> parameter (e.g., allow high correlation if the existing position is already +2R in profit and trailing).</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F2 🔴 Live Data Latency in Sentinel</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Current state:</span></strong><span class=""> </span>`scripts/run_sentinel.py`<span class=""> calls </span>`yf.download(..., period="1d", interval="15m")`<span class="">. On yfinance free tier, intraday 15m data is delayed by up to 15 minutes and often stale by an entire bar.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why it matters:</span></strong><span class=""> The trailing stop sentinel is the </span><em><span class="">only</span></em><span class=""> automated line of defence between a paper position and a catastrophic gap-down. If it's operating on stale data, it will trigger exits 15 minutes late — which in a micro-cap can mean -8% instead of -5.5%.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Fix:</span></strong></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">You already have an authenticated Fyers client (</span>`src/ingestion/fyers_client.py`<span class="">). </span>`fyers_client.get_live_quotes()`<span class=""> returns </span><strong><span class="">real-time LTP</span></strong><span class=""> for free with the Fyers free data plan.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Rewrite </span>`run_sentinel.py`<span class=""> to use </span>`fyers_client.get_live_quotes(symbols)`<span class=""> for LTP + </span>`fyers_client.get_market_depth(symbol)`<span class=""> for high/low if the API supports it (falls back to </span>`yf.download`<span class=""> on auth failure with a loud Telegram alert).</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Set </span>`yfinance`<span class=""> as the explicit </span><strong><span class="">degraded fallback</span></strong><span class=""> with the alert </span>`"⚠️ Sentinel running on delayed data — increased gap risk"`<span class=""> so the operator knows.</span></p></li></ul><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> Fyers free tier gives real-time NSE LTP with no per-request cost. It's the single biggest reliability win available at $0.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> If Fyers revokes the free data plan, the system must gracefully degrade. The current </span>`yf`<span class=""> path becomes the fallback.</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F3 🔴 ML Methodology Gaps</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">The current pipeline uses a single 80/20 time split, a 6-feature binary target, and no class-balance handling. This is fragile.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Concrete upgrades, ranked by impact:</span></strong></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">(a) Purged K-Fold CV with Embargo (López de Prado).</span></strong><br><span class="">Replace the single split in </span>`run_model_training.py`<span class=""> with 5-fold </span>`PurgedKFold(n_splits=5, embargo=5)`<span class="">. The embargo of 5 days matches the forward-return horizon and eliminates the label-leakage that survives even in time-series splits (because </span>`t+5`<span class=""> labels of the training fold overlap with </span>`t`<span class=""> features of the test fold). This is not optional — the current setup has </span><strong><span class="">look-ahead leakage</span></strong><span class="">.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">(b) Triple-Barrier Labeling.</span></strong><br><span class="">Replace </span>`y = (fwd_ret &gt; 1.5%)`<span class=""> with the López de Prado triple-barrier method: </span>`+1`<span class=""> if upper barrier (2×ATR) hit first, </span>`-1`<span class=""> if lower barrier (1.5×ATR) hit first, </span>`0`<span class=""> if time barrier (5 days) hit first. This matches the actual trade management logic (stop-loss, target, timeout) far better than a flat 1.5% threshold. Currently the model is trained to predict a </span><em><span class="">different</span></em><span class=""> thing than what the trading system does.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">(c) Class Imbalance Handling.</span></strong><br><span class="">Compute </span>`scale_pos_weight = neg_count / pos_count`<span class=""> in the XGBoost objective. Add early stopping on a validation fold. Current setup trains with no imbalance handling, which biases the model toward the majority class (bearish labels dominate in most regimes).</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">(d) Meta-Labeling (Optional but Powerful).</span></strong><br><span class="">Train a second binary classifier </span>`P(primary prediction is correct | features)`<span class="">. Use it to </span><em><span class="">size down</span></em><span class=""> rather than filter — the primary model fires the trade, the meta-model scales the conviction. This gives the LLM debate a quantitative counterpart.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> These are the industry-standard techniques for financial ML, they cost nothing, they run in seconds, and they directly address known failure modes (leakage, mislabeled target, class imbalance).</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> Requires refactoring the training script; ~4–6 hours of work; needs an updated model artifact format. The 0.53 AUC gate may need to be raised to ~0.55 because a properly cross-validated model is harder to overfit.</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F4 🟠 Backtest Cost Model Missing</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Current state:</span></strong><span class=""> </span>`research/backtest_harness.py`<span class=""> applies no Indian transaction costs. Real Indian equity round-trip costs on delivery are roughly:</span></p><div class="ds-scroll-area ds-scroll-area--show-on-focus-within ds-scroll-area--enabled _1210dd7 c03cafe9" style="--container-height: 365px;"><div class="ds-scroll-area__gutters" style="--container-height: 365px; position: sticky; top: 0px; left: 0px; right: 0px; width: 100%; height: 0px;"><div class="ds-scroll-area__horizontal-gutter" style="left: 0px; right: 0px; display: block; top: calc(var(--container-height) - 14px); height: 8px;"><div class="ds-scroll-area__horizontal-bar" style="display: none;"></div></div><div class="ds-scroll-area__vertical-gutter" style="right: 3px; top: 3px; bottom: calc(0px - var(--container-height) + 3px); width: 8px;"><div class="ds-scroll-area__vertical-bar" style="display: none;"></div></div></div><table><thead><tr><th><span class="">Component</span></th><th><span class="">Rate</span></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><span class="">Brokerage (discount)</span></td><td><span class="">₹20 per order (flat)</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">STT (delivery sell)</span></td><td><span class="">0.1% on sell value</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">Exchange txn charge</span></td><td><span class="">~0.00325% per side</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">SEBI turnover fee</span></td><td><span class="">₹10 per crore</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">Stamp duty (buy)</span></td><td><span class="">0.015%</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">GST on brokerage + txn</span></td><td><span class="">18%</span></td></tr><tr><td><strong><span class="">Total effective</span></strong></td><td><strong><span class="">~0.15%–0.25% per round trip</span></strong></td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Fix:</span></strong><span class=""> Add a </span>`costs.py`<span class=""> module and subtract from returns in the backtest harness. Also model slippage as a function of order size / ADTV: </span>`slippage_bps = k * sqrt(order_size / ADTV) * spread_bps`<span class=""> (the square-root market-impact law), with </span>`k ≈ 0.5–1.0`<span class="">.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> Without this, a strategy that appears to earn +1.5% per round-trip can be flat-to-negative net of costs. This is the #1 reason retail backtests lie.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> Some signals will drop below breakeven — that is the point of the exercise. No risk.</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F5 🟠 Crude Regime Detection</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`market_regime = 1 if NIFTY &gt; SMA50 else 0`<span class=""> is a blunt instrument. It fails during:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Whipsaw markets crossing the SMA every few days</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Volatility spikes that don't move price trend (2018 volmageddon style)</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Sector rotation regimes where the index is flat but leadership rotates</span></p></li></ul><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Better alternatives, in increasing sophistication:</span></strong></p><ol start="1"><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">VIX Term Structure Ratio</span></strong><span class=""> (free): </span>`VIX_9D / VIX_30D`<span class="">. Backwardation (&lt; 1) is a strong near-term fear indicator. Requires </span>`^VIX9D`<span class=""> from yfinance.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Gaussian HMM with 3 states</span></strong><span class=""> on (daily return, realized vol) — 30 lines of code with </span>`hmmlearn`<span class="">. States: calm-trend, choppy, high-vol-crisis. Fail-closed routing: only allow full size in "calm-trend".</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Bayesian Online Change Point Detection (BOCPD)</span></strong><span class=""> — detects regime shifts in real time with a probabilistic posterior, works well with 2-3 years of data.</span></p></li></ol><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Recommendation:</span></strong><span class=""> Ship #1 immediately (1 hour of work), then #2 as an upgrade.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> These indicators respond to </span><em><span class="">volatility</span></em><span class=""> regimes, not just </span><em><span class="">price level</span></em><span class="">. They will prevent entries the day before a crash.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> HMM needs stable training data and can flip states occasionally — use a hysteresis rule (</span>`must be in state for ≥3 days before acting`<span class="">).</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F6 🟠 Narrow Feature Set</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">The XGBoost model currently uses: </span>`sharpe_rank, dist_high, market_regime, rel_rsi, ema_dist, vol_cluster`<span class="">.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Missing features (all free to compute from existing Bhavcopy):</span></strong></p><div class="ds-scroll-area ds-scroll-area--show-on-focus-within ds-scroll-area--enabled _1210dd7 c03cafe9" style="--container-height: 436px;"><div class="ds-scroll-area__gutters" style="--container-height: 436px; position: sticky; top: 0px; left: 0px; right: 0px; width: 100%; height: 0px;"><div class="ds-scroll-area__horizontal-gutter" style="left: 0px; right: 0px; display: block; top: calc(var(--container-height) - 14px); height: 8px;"><div class="ds-scroll-area__horizontal-bar" style="display: none;"></div></div><div class="ds-scroll-area__vertical-gutter" style="right: 3px; top: 3px; bottom: calc(0px - var(--container-height) + 3px); width: 8px;"><div class="ds-scroll-area__vertical-bar" style="display: none;"></div></div></div><table><thead><tr><th><span class="">Feature</span></th><th><span class="">Rationale</span></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><strong><span class="">Amihud illiquidity</span></strong><span class=""> </span>`|ret| / turnover`<span class=""> (20d mean)</span></td><td><span class="">Captures price impact risk better than ADTV alone</span></td></tr><tr><td><strong><span class="">Delivery % trend</span></strong><span class=""> (5d vs 20d)</span></td><td><span class="">Distinguishes genuine accumulation from operator churn — currently only used in trap shield</span></td></tr><tr><td><strong><span class="">Volume-weighted skewness</span></strong></td><td><span class="">Identifies distribution days</span></td></tr><tr><td><strong><span class="">Intraday range ratio</span></strong><span class=""> </span>`(H-L)/C`</td><td><span class="">Volatility-of-volatility proxy</span></td></tr><tr><td><strong><span class="">Upper circuit hit count</span></strong><span class=""> (last 20 days)</span></td><td><span class="">Micro-cap "operator momentum" signal</span></td></tr><tr><td><strong><span class="">Cross-sectional rank of ADTV</span></strong></td><td><span class="">Relative liquidity within today's universe</span></td></tr><tr><td><strong><span class="">Close position in daily range</span></strong><span class=""> </span>`(C-L)/(H-L)`</td><td><span class="">Classic smart-money footprint</span></td></tr><tr><td><strong><span class="">Sector-relative 20-day return</span></strong></td><td><span class="">Isolates idiosyncratic momentum</span></td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> Feature engineering is the highest-ROI activity in financial ML. Adding 8 parsimonious features to a shallow tree should raise out-of-sample AUC by 2–5 bps in absolute terms without materially increasing overfitting risk (given proper CV).</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> Every new feature must be recomputed for both training and live. Add a </span><strong><span class="">feature store</span></strong><span class=""> (see F11) so training and serving cannot drift.</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F7 🟠 Exit Asymmetry</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Two issues:</span></p><ol start="1"><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Chandelier Exit vs Fixed ATR Trail.</span></strong><span class=""> The current trailing stop is </span>`peak - 1.5×ATR(14)`<span class="">. Chandelier Exit uses </span>`peak - 3×ATR(22)`<span class=""> and is more robust to intraday noise; the Parabolic SAR is even more conservative near trend ends. Consider adding an </span><strong><span class="">adaptive multiplier</span></strong><span class=""> </span>`k = f(regime, days_in_trade)`<span class="">:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Early trade (day 1–5): tighter (1.3×ATR)</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Trending (day 5–20): wider (2.0×ATR)</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Mature (&gt;20 days): parabolic acceleration</span></p></li></ul></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">No time stop.</span></strong><span class=""> A position that hasn't moved ±0.5R in 15 days is a dead position tying up capital. Add: </span>`if days_in_trade &gt; 15 and abs(PnL) &lt; 0.5R → exit at market`<span class="">.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">No earnings blackout.</span></strong><span class=""> yfinance free gives </span>`ticker.calendar`<span class=""> with earnings date. Reject new entries within 3 days of earnings; tighten trail to </span>`peak - 1×ATR`<span class=""> for open positions the day before earnings.</span></p></li></ol><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> Reduces the "dud trades" that tie up the 12% max position slot. Earnings blackout alone can prevent dozens of catastrophic gap-downs.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> Time stops can exit just before a delayed breakout. Use a soft penalty (reduce size 50%) rather than hard exit if the pattern still looks constructive.</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F8 🟡 LLM Debate Depth</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Current graph: </span>`bull → bear → judge`<span class="">, one pass, free-text. This is fine for the current volume but has obvious uplift potential:</span></p><ol start="1"><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Structured outputs.</span></strong><span class=""> Force the judge to return JSON: </span>`{synthesis, conviction_score, key_risk_flag, catalyst_strength}`<span class="">. Removes regex fragility (the current code even has a fallback when regex fails — a signal the free-text pipeline is fragile).</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Self-consistency sampling.</span></strong><span class=""> Sample the judge 3× at temperature 0.4 and take the </span><strong><span class="">median conviction score</span></strong><span class="">. Costs 3× tokens but reduces variance in the decision variable. Alternatively, run the debate twice with </span><strong><span class="">different LLMs</span></strong><span class=""> (Gemini Flash + Groq Llama-3.3) and require </span><em><span class="">agreement within 2 conviction points</span></em><span class=""> — otherwise escalate to Gemini Pro.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">RAG over earnings transcripts.</span></strong><span class=""> </span><a href="https://screener.in/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer"><span class="">Screener.in</span></a><span class=""> often links to concall transcripts. A local FAISS index (free) + a small embedding model (</span>`BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5`<span class="">, ~130MB) run on the Oracle VM lets the Bull agent cite </span><em><span class="">management commentary</span></em><span class=""> rather than just financial ratios. This is a step-change in the quality of the qualitative layer.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Adversarial chain:</span></strong><span class=""> Add a </span>`regulatory_risk_node`<span class=""> that specifically searches for SEBI orders, ASM/GSM escalations, and promoter pledge news via a tiny RSS aggregator (free).</span></p></li></ol><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> Currently the LLM layer adds ~$0 but also ~0 signal — it mostly restates the deterministic arbiter's numbers. RAG and structured outputs make it an </span><em><span class="">orthogonal</span></em><span class=""> information source.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> Token costs rise (still $0 on free tiers, but slower). RAG indexing adds ~10 minutes per company per week.</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F9 🟡 Statistical Validation Missing</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">The current live system reports live win-rate/Sharpe but doesn't correct for </span><strong><span class="">multiple testing</span></strong><span class="">. If you try 20 strategy variants and pick the best, your "5% significant" result is actually ~64% likely to be spurious (López de Prado, </span><em><span class="">Deflated Sharpe Ratio</span></em><span class="">).</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Add to the post-mortem lab:</span></strong></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Deflated Sharpe Ratio (DSR):</span></strong><span class=""> Corrects observed Sharpe for number of trials and skew/kurtosis.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Probability of Backtest Overfitting (PBO)</span></strong><span class=""> via combinatorially symmetric cross-validation.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">White's Reality Check</span></strong><span class=""> for pairwise strategy comparison.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Stationary Bootstrap</span></strong><span class=""> (block length ≥ forward-return horizon) for confidence intervals on returns.</span></p></li></ul><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> Prevents the classic trap of deploying a strategy that looks great in-sample because it was selected from many candidates.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> More complex reporting. Implement in </span>`scripts/run_evaluator.py`<span class=""> as part of the monthly report.</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F10 🟡 Position Sizing</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">The current rule is fixed-fractional 1.5% risk per trade, 12% cap per position. This is safe but </span><strong><span class="">suboptimal</span></strong><span class="">:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Fractional Kelly (f/4):</span></strong><span class=""> </span>`f* = (p·b - q)/b`<span class=""> where </span>`p`<span class=""> = win rate, </span>`b`<span class=""> = win/loss ratio. Use quarter-Kelly for safety. This is provably optimal for geometric growth.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">CVaR-based sizing:</span></strong><span class=""> Size = </span>`risk_budget / CVaR_5%`<span class=""> where CVaR is computed from the stock's historical tail. This is more conservative than ATR alone and handles fat tails.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Volatility targeting at portfolio level:</span></strong><span class=""> Scale total book exposure so portfolio realized vol targets, say, 15% annualised. Freeze new entries when realized vol &gt; 22%.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Risk Parity across sectors:</span></strong><span class=""> Ensure sector exposure × sector vol contributes equally to total portfolio vol.</span></p></li></ul><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> Fixed-fractional leaves money on the table during high-edge periods and over-risks during noisy periods. Vol targeting in particular is one of the few free lunches in portfolio construction.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> More moving parts, more parameters to overfit. Start with vol-targeting only (single parameter).</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F11 🟡 Operational Resilience</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">No off-box backup.</span></strong><span class=""> If the Oracle VM dies, </span>`alphasentinel.duckdb`<span class=""> is gone. Fixes:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Nightly </span>`scp`<span class="">/</span>`rclone`<span class=""> snapshot to Backblaze B2 (10 GB free tier) or Cloudflare R2 (10 GB free).</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Keep 30 daily + 12 monthly snapshots.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Test restore once a month via a cron that spins up a fresh DB and verifies a checksum.</span></p></li></ul><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Log rotation exists</span></strong><span class=""> (</span>`logrotate_snippet.conf`<span class="">) but isn't installed by the deployment runbook — verify it's part of Phase 6.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">No health endpoint.</span></strong><span class=""> The Streamlit dashboard is the only monitor. Add </span>`/api/health`<span class=""> on a FastAPI sidecar (already in </span>`requirements-lock.txt`<span class="">) that reports: last successful bhavcopy date, last sentinel run, LLM waterfall health, WAL size.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> The system currently has no recovery story for the most likely failure mode (VM loss).</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> Almost none. B2 and R2 both have free tiers.</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F12 🟡 Shadow / A-B Framework</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`SHADOW_MODE`<span class=""> exists in settings but I don't see it used consistently. Recommend:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`positions`<span class=""> table gets a </span>`strategy_version`<span class=""> column.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Each screener run generates trades under </span><strong><span class="">two model versions</span></strong><span class=""> (champion + challenger), one live, one shadow-only.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Monthly comparison uses the DSR from F9 to promote challenger to champion only when statistically significant.</span></p></li></ul><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> Enables disciplined strategy iteration instead of ad-hoc code changes.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> Doubles the LLM debate token consumption during shadow runs; keep shadow LLM calls off (reuse champion's debate for the challenger signal, only re-run risk arbiter).</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F13 🟢 Corporate Action Minor Gaps</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Two small issues:</span></p><ol start="1"><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong>`vcp_screener.py`<span class=""> uses </span>`close_df.tail(252).max()`</strong><span class=""> for the 52-week high. Minervini's template uses </span><strong><span class="">intraday high</span></strong><span class="">, not close. Fix: pivot on </span>`high_price`<span class="">.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong>`anti_trap_shield.py`<span class=""> reads historical Bhavcopy after corporate action adjustment, but doesn't account for a split </span><em><span class="">during the trade</span></em><span class="">.</span></strong><span class=""> The position sizing was already handled in </span>`corporate_actions.py`<span class="">, but the R-multiple math (</span>`target_1 = trigger + 3×ATR`<span class="">) uses pre-split ATR. If a split happens mid-trade, the R-multiple becomes meaningless. Fix: on split, reset </span>`target_1`<span class="">, </span>`target_2`<span class="">, and </span>`stop_loss`<span class=""> from the </span><strong><span class="">post-split entry price</span></strong><span class=""> and a fresh ATR.</span></p></li></ol><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> Prevents the R-multiple logic from silently becoming wrong on split events.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> None.</span></p><hr><h3><span class="">F14 🟢 News Sentiment Layer</span></h3><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Currently only </span>`recent_news_count`<span class=""> is used. A proper free sentiment layer:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Pull RSS from Moneycontrol / ET Markets / Business Standard (all have free RSS feeds).</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Run </span><strong><span class="">FinBERT</span></strong><span class=""> locally (HuggingFace </span>`yiyanghkust/finbert-tone`<span class="">, ~440MB) on CPU with a batch queue once per hour.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Feed a </span>`sentiment_score ∈ [-1, 1]`<span class=""> and </span>`event_type`<span class=""> (earnings, regulatory, M&amp;A) into the Bull/Bear prompts.</span></p></li></ul><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Why better:</span></strong><span class=""> Adds orthogonal qualitative signal at $0 marginal cost.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Trade-off:</span></strong><span class=""> CPU cost on the Oracle free tier; run it off-hours and cache.</span></p><hr><h2><span class="">3. Assumptions That Are Still Valid vs. Now Questionable</span></h2><div class="ds-scroll-area ds-scroll-area--show-on-focus-within ds-scroll-area--enabled _1210dd7 c03cafe9" style="--container-height: 794px;"><div class="ds-scroll-area__gutters" style="--container-height: 794px; add earnings blackout (F7)</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">DuckDB single-writer with lock is enough</span></td><td><span class="">⚠️ Partial</span></td><td><span class="">Correct, but no off-box backup (F11)</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">yfinance intraday is real-time enough for sentinel</span></td><td><span class="">❌ </span><strong><span class="">FALSE</span></strong></td><td><span class="">See F2 — the most urgent operational risk</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">DuckDB WAL won't explode</span></td><td><span class="">✅ Valid</span></td><td>`run_db_maintenance.py`<span class=""> handles checkpoint</span></td></tr><tr><td>`is_system_halted()`<span class=""> fail-closed is correct</span></td><td><span class="">✅ Valid</span></td><td><span class="">Good defensive default</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">Streamlit + Tailscale is sufficient monitoring</span></td><td><span class="">⚠️ Partial</span></td><td><span class="">Add /health endpoint (F11)</span></td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Missed in prior audit:</span></strong><span class=""> F1 (correlation control), F2 (sentinel latency), F3 (CV leakage), F7 (time stop), F12 (A/B), F13 (52W high uses close).</span></p><hr><h2><span class="">4. What </span><em><span class="">Not</span></em><span class=""> to Do</span></h2><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Some commonly-recommended techniques would hurt this system:</span></p><div class="ds-scroll-area ds-scroll-area--show-on-focus-within ds-scroll-area--enabled _1210dd7 c03cafe9" style="--container-height: 611px;"><div class="ds-scroll-area__gutters" style="--container-height: 611px; width: 8px;"><div class="ds-scroll-area__vertical-bar" style="display: none;"></div></div></div><table><thead><tr><th><span class="">Rejected Idea</span></th><th><span class="">Why</span></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><span class="">Replace DuckDB with PostgreSQL</span></td><td><span class="">Loses columnar scanning speed on 2M+ rows; adds ops complexity; $0 budget doesn't survive managed PG</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">Add intraday derivatives / options hedging</span></td><td><span class="">Explicitly out of scope in PRD; adds margin calls and requires SEBI registration for automated F&amp;O</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">Use LLM as a </span><em><span class="">sizer</span></em></td><td><span class="">Destroys the entire deterministic-risk design philosophy</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">Deploy microservices</span></td><td><span class="">Over-engineering for a single-developer, single-VM setup</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">Use Kubernetes</span></td><td><span class="">Same as above</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">Migrate to paid data (Bloomberg/Refinitiv)</span></td><td><span class="">Violates the $0 mandate and doesn't fix the real gaps</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">Train deep learning models (LSTM/Transformer)</span></td><td><span class="">With &lt;10 years of daily data and ~1,500 symbols, tree ensembles consistently outperform; risk of overfit is severe</span></td></tr><tr><td><span class="">Add live broker order routing now</span></td><td><span class="">Paper trading gate exists for a reason; respect it</span></td></tr></tbody></table></div><hr><h2><span class="">6. Risk / Trade-Off Notes for Each Accepted Improvement</span></h2><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">F2 (Fyers sentinel):</span></strong><span class=""> Depends on Fyers free tier continuing to provide REST quotes. Keep yfinance fallback; add explicit "degraded data" Telegram alert so the operator can manually intervene.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">F3 (purged CV):</span></strong><span class=""> Model AUC will likely </span><em><span class="">drop</span></em><span class=""> initially (to ~0.52–0.55) because leakage is removed. Do not panic — this is the true score. Raise the persistence gate accordingly.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">F1 (correlation guard):</span></strong><span class=""> Will reject some profitable setups in concentrated bull sectors (e.g., defence 2023). Soften with an "approved override" flow: if the new trade is same sector but existing is already trailing +2R, allow 50% size.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">F4 (cost model):</span></strong><span class=""> Some backtests that looked profitable will become marginal. That is the correct outcome; without it, the system over-trades.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">F7 (earnings blackout):</span></strong><span class=""> Blocking entries 3 days before earnings will occasionally miss pre-earnings runups. Trade-off accepted — the tail risk from a miss is far larger than the missed upside.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">F10 (vol targeting):</span></strong><span class=""> Requires accurate realized vol measurement. Use 20-day EWMA of portfolio returns; be careful not to over-de-risk during legitimate drawdowns (add hysteresis: only throttle if 10-day realized vol &gt; 2× its 6-month median).</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">F14 (FinBERT):</span></strong><span class=""> Adds ~440MB model + inference time. Run off-hours to avoid slowing the 3:15 PM pipeline.</span></p></li></ul><hr><h2><span class="">7. Suggested Rollout Plan</span></h2><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Sprint A (Week 1 — "Stop the bleeding"):</span></strong><span class=""></span><br><span class="">F2, F3a, F3b, F3c, F1, F4.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Sprint B (Week 2 — "Sharpen the signals"):</span></strong><span class=""></span><br><span class="">F5a, F6, F7a, F7b, F7c, F11a.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Sprint C (Week 3 — "Rigour"):</span></strong><span class=""></span><br><span class="">F8a, F9, F10.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Sprint D (Month 2 — "Moats"):</span></strong><span class=""></span><br><span class="">F5b, F8c, F12, F14, F13, F13b.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Every change must be validated via the paper-trading 30-day gate before it affects live capital decisions. Use the DSR/PBO machinery from F9 to arbitrate between current champion and challenger.</span></p><hr><h2><span class="">8. Code-Level Observations (Opportunistic Fixes)</span></h2><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Beyond the strategic items above, a scan of the code surfaced these micro-issues worth closing during adjacent work:</span></p><ol start="1"><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`src/screening/ml_features.py::_get_benchmark_data()`<span class=""> uses a </span><strong><span class="">module-level global cache</span></strong><span class=""> without a lock — a race condition in multithreaded contexts. Wrap with </span>`threading.Lock()`<span class=""> or use </span>`functools.lru_cache`<span class="">.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`src/screening/vcp_screener.py`<span class=""> — the 52W high uses </span>`close_df.tail(252).max()`<span class="">. Should be </span>`high_df.tail(252).max()`<span class=""> to match Minervini's definition.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`src/risk/arbiter.py`<span class=""> — the sector cap query includes today's </span>`PENDING_REVIEW`<span class=""> candidates, but the arbiter is called </span><em><span class="">before</span></em><span class=""> the candidate is inserted. The check therefore doesn't see the current candidate; add +1 to the count for the current evaluation or perform the INSERT first.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`src/ingestion/bhavcopy.py`<span class=""> — the fallback synthesises upper/lower circuit as </span>`prev_close × (1 ± band%)`<span class="">, but if the previous close is missing it uses the day's </span><em><span class="">open</span></em><span class="">. On gap-up days this produces incorrect circuit bands. Prefer to skip band synthesis (leave NULL) and let the </span>`arbiter.py`<span class=""> default to 20% for EQ and 5% for BE/BZ/SM — which it already does.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`scripts/run_live_preview.py`<span class=""> — the sentiment/quality loop passes </span>`live_prices`<span class=""> and </span>`live_volumes`<span class=""> but for symbols without yfinance data it silently degrades to using yesterday's close. Add a </span>`data_quality_flag`<span class=""> so downstream filter logic knows when it's operating on stale inputs.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`src/agents/debate_graph.py`<span class=""> — when the regex to extract </span>`CONVICTION_SCORE`<span class=""> fails, the code defaults to </span>`5.0`<span class="">. Because the consensus gate requires ≥7.0, this means </span><strong><span class="">any</span></strong><span class=""> parsing failure silently vetoes the trade. That's fail-closed (good) but it hides a bug. Add explicit logging with the raw text and a Prometheus-style counter to track parsing failure rate.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`scripts/run_scheduler.py`<span class=""> — </span>`schedule.every(15).minutes.do(job_sentinel)`<span class=""> fires at every 15-minute mark regardless of market hours; the guard is inside </span>`job_sentinel`<span class="">, which is correct, but </span>`is_market_hours_ist()`<span class=""> doesn't check NSE holidays. Import and use </span>`is_nse_holiday()`<span class=""> so the sentinel doesn't fire on Republic Day.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`src/utils/holidays.py`<span class=""> — the </span>`HARDCODED_NSE_HOLIDAYS`<span class=""> list only covers 2026. Add a startup check that warns if the current year is missing and no API-based refresh has succeeded in 30 days.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`src/db/session.py::get_read_connection()`<span class=""> — the WAL-detected branch opens a write connection to checkpoint, then re-opens read-only. There's a small window between the two where another process could acquire the write lock and re-create a WAL. Rare but possible; add a bounded retry loop around the second read-only open.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`src/ingestion/screener_scraper.py`<span class=""> — </span>`interest_income_ratio`<span class=""> is computed as </span>`other_income / sales`<span class="">, but "other income" in </span><a href="https://screener.in/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer"><span class="">Screener.in</span></a><span class=""> includes interest income, forex gains, and misc. The PRD calls for </span>`max(other_income, interest_income)`<span class=""> semantics. Align the scraper with the PRD's formula or the Shariah gate may pass non-compliant names.</span></p></li></ol><hr><h2><span class="">9. Final Verdict</span></h2><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">The system is </span><strong><span class="">architecturally strong but methodologically incomplete</span></strong><span class="">. The 19-item SDLC audit fixed bugs; this audit reveals that the deeper issue is not correctness — it is </span><strong><span class="">missing layers of analysis</span></strong><span class=""> at exactly the places that determine long-run profitability:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Portfolio construction</span></strong><span class=""> (F1, F10) — currently absent.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Statistical rigour in ML training</span></strong><span class=""> (F3, F9) — currently absent.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Realistic backtesting</span></strong><span class=""> (F4) — currently absent.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Operational resilience</span></strong><span class=""> (F2, F11) — partially present, but the sentinel data feed is a single point of failure.</span></p></li></ul><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Fixing F1–F4 alone would place this system in the top 1% of retail quant stacks. Fixing F5–F10 in addition would make it genuinely competitive with small institutional funds, at </span><strong><span class="">$0 incremental cost</span></strong><span class="">.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">The system is not optimal. But it is close enough to being optimal </span><em><span class="">that the remaining gaps are worth closing now, before paper trading converts to live capital</span></em><span class="">.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Recommended immediate action:</span></strong><span class=""> Launch Sprint A this week. Re-audit after 30 days of paper trading with the DSR/PBO machinery (F9) in place.</span></p><hr><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><em><span class="">End of report. Total findings: 14 major + 10 micro. Recommended for immediate action: F1, F2, F3a/b/c, F4.</span></em></p>

But if we process B first (the loop order isn't guaranteed), then B's update only touches dates before B's ex_date, which includes dates before A's ex_date too. Then A's update touches dates before A's ex_date (which is a subset). Then A: dates d &lt; A.ex_date get × A.mult. Since d &lt; A.ex_date, it gets × A.mult additional. So d gets B×A. Correct.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">OK commutative, no issue.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">But wait - what if the record_corporate_action is called with already-applied actions from a previous run? The </span>`is_applied = FALSE`<span class=""> filter prevents re-application. Fine.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">However, there's an important flaw: </span>`record_corporate_action`<span class=""> uses </span>`ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING`<span class="">. If yfinance re-detects the same split on subsequent runs, no problem. But if the caller passes a different ex_date (e.g., yfinance provides a slightly different date), the id changes, and a new action is recorded - double-adjustment. Unlikely but possible.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Screener scraper</span></strong><span class="">: The interest_income_ratio uses </span>`other_income`<span class=""> which lumps together actual interest income, forex gains, other income. However, the fallback returns 0.0 which is fine.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Actually wait - the row matching uses </span>`if row_name.lower() in tds[0].text.lower()`<span class="">. So searching for </span>`'sales'`<span class=""> matches </span>`'Sales'`<span class=""> but also </span>`'Sales Growth %'`<span class=""> or any row containing "sales". Actually </span><span class="">Screener.in</span><span class=""> has both "Sales" and "Sales Growth" typically. The one that matches first wins. Since we iterate through </span>`section.find_all('tr')`<span class="">, order matters. Not necessarily a bug but fragile. Better to use exact match or check the row begins with the target.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Similarly for 'borrowings' - Screener may show "Borrowings" and the row containing "Borrowings" appears as expected. But there's also "Total Borrowings" in some cases. Exact prefix match would be safer.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Macro feeds</span></strong><span class="">: </span>`yf.Tickers("^VIX ^GSPC BZ=F USDINR=X ^NSEI")`<span class=""> - the </span>`^GSPC`<span class=""> on yfinance has a </span>`history(period="5d")`<span class=""> - OK. But there's a subtle issue: </span>`.history(period="5d")`<span class=""> returns the last 5 calendar days of data, and for weekends/holidays you may only get 3-4 rows. The </span>`.iloc[-2]`<span class=""> and </span>`.iloc[-1]`<span class=""> might be a bit fragile if it's Monday morning and only Friday's data is available - then p0 == p1 and change is 0. Fine.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">The macro_weather_score formula is ad-hoc. Something like: 4 penalty rules summed. That's fine but not principled. Consider z-scoring instead.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Fyers client</span></strong><span class="">:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`_load_cached_token()`<span class=""> reads from env var. There's no OAuth flow. Token refresh isn't implemented. So the fallback to yfinance will silently kick in when the token expires (Fyers tokens expire at 6 AM IST daily).</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`get_market_depth`<span class=""> requires a paid Fyers plan (Level 2 data) - actually the free plan gives Level 1 (top-of-book) so </span>`depth`<span class=""> should work but only bid[0]/ask[0]. OK.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`is_market_open`<span class=""> calls the API but the API returns </span>`CLOSED`<span class=""> for pre-market and post-market. Fine.</span></p></li></ul></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">tradingview.py</span></strong><span class="">:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`TA_Handler`<span class=""> returns </span>`recommendation`<span class=""> which is like </span>`"BUY"`<span class="">, </span>`"STRONG_BUY"`<span class=""> etc. Fine.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">But there's no caching. Every screener run queries TradingView for each candidate. Rate limits may kick in. Add a small TTL cache (5 minutes).</span></p></li></ul></li></ol><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Layer 2: Screening</span></strong></p><ol start="7"><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">liquidity_guard.py</span></strong><span class="">:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">The </span>`MIN_CIRCUIT_BAND_PCT`<span class=""> check uses </span>`circuit_band_pct &lt;= settings.MIN_CIRCUIT_BAND_PCT`<span class=""> for </span>`has_circuit_fill_risk`<span class="">, and then the actual fail is for </span>`&lt;= 2`<span class="">. This is inconsistent: a 5% band stock is flagged as risky but not rejected. Then it's handled by the arbiter (halve size). OK by design.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">The ADTV uses </span>`MEDIAN(...)`<span class=""> over the last 30 days, but the tier threshold is named </span>`adtv_20d`<span class=""> - slight naming inconsistency.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">If the stock has only 5 days of data, </span>`data_days &lt; 5`<span class=""> returns fail. But the check is </span>`&lt; 5`<span class="">, so exactly 5 days passes. Fine.</span></p></li></ul></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">vcp_screener.py</span></strong><span class="">:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Looking at the vectorized logic more carefully: </span>`sma_50 = close_df.rolling(50, min_periods=50).mean()`<span class=""> - strict 50 days. Then </span>`c0 = current_price &gt; sma_50[sym].iloc[-1]`<span class="">. If current price comes from live tick, but sma_50 is from previous close, there's an implicit assumption that today's close is a good proxy - fine.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Live tick integration</span></strong><span class="">: </span>`live_prices.get(sym)`<span class=""> is used as </span>`current_price`<span class=""> but the SMA is computed without the live tick. This means on a big up day, the SMA and price will be inconsistent - though since SMA is slow-moving, effect is small.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`high_252d = close_df.tail(252).max()`<span class=""> - uses CLOSE, not HIGH. This is the F13 issue I noted. Should be </span>`high_df.tail(252).max()`<span class="">.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`low_252d = close_df.tail(252).min()`<span class=""> - uses CLOSE, not LOW. Same issue.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`volatility_contraction = volatility_10d &lt; (volatility_30d * 0.75)`<span class=""> - this is a VCP proxy but very crude. Real VCP looks for multiple progressive contractions (e.g., -20%, -12%, -6%.). Could be improved.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`rs_score = (stock_return - benchmark_rs_data) * 100`<span class="">. </span>`benchmark_rs_data = (bench_close.iloc[-1] - bench_close.iloc[-rs_period]) / bench_close.iloc[-rs_period]`<span class="">. The benchmark uses its own last date, not necessarily aligned with the stock's last date. If a stock hasn't traded in a while (halt), misalignment occurs.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Regime bypass rule</span></strong><span class="">: PRD says "If the broader market is in a correction, a stock is allowed to proceed if and only if RS Score &gt; 15.0 and volume dry-up is confirmed." But the code just does </span>`if not regime_passed: continue`<span class=""> - it skips the RS bypass rule entirely! This is a </span><strong><span class="">PRD compliance gap</span></strong><span class="">. This was not noted in the prior audit.</span></p></li></ul></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">anti_trap_shield.py</span></strong><span class="">:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Layer 2 Climax check: uses </span>`df["high_price"].iloc[-1]`<span class=""> and </span>`df["low_price"].iloc[-1]`<span class=""> (yesterday's data if live). But if live_price is provided, it does </span>`high_price = max(high_price, live_price)`<span class="">. Doesn't use live high/low ticks. Actually the caller can pass live_price but not live_high/live_low. So the intraday wick detection uses yesterday's candle + today's live price as a proxy. This is imprecise.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Layer 6 FOMO: </span>`recent_news_count &gt;= 3 AND run_3d_pct &gt; 15`<span class="">. Reasonable but arbitrary.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">There's a subtle issue: on a fresh entry, </span>`live_price`<span class=""> may not equal </span>`trigger_price`<span class="">. But the trap check uses </span>`current_price = live_price if live_price is not None else close[-1]`<span class=""> and </span>`pivot_price = candidate["trigger_price"]`<span class="">. So if live price is below trigger, </span>`pivot_extension_pct`<span class=""> is negative and passes. Fine.</span></p></li></ul></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">ml_predictor.py</span></strong><span class="">:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Uses </span>`LIMIT 300`<span class=""> in the query - good, updated from previous LIMIT 60.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`df['trade_date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['trade_date']).dt.date`<span class=""> - converts to date objects. Then </span>`engineer_live_tick`<span class=""> checks </span>`pd.to_datetime(df['trade_date'].iloc[-1]).normalize() == today_ts`<span class="">. Fine.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">But: if </span>`live_price`<span class=""> is None, </span>`engineer_live_tick`<span class=""> returns df unmodified. So inference uses last historical close. Fine for a diagnostic.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Missing</span></strong><span class="">: No check that historical data ends near the current date. If a stock has been halted for 30 days, the model will happily give a prediction on stale data. Add: </span>`if (today - last_trade_date).days &gt; 5: return 0.0`<span class="">.</span></p></li></ul></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">second_opinion_gate.py</span></strong><span class=""> - same concern as ml_predictor.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">mean_reversion_screener.py</span></strong><span class="">:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Uses simple rolling mean RSI, not Wilder's exponential. The PRD specifies Wilder's. Mismatch with spec but not fatal.</span></p></li></ul></li></ol><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Layer 3: ML</span></strong></p><ol start="13"><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">ml_features.py</span></strong><span class="">:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`_get_benchmark_data()`<span class=""> uses a global cache - no lock. Race condition if called from multiple threads. In practice single-threaded, but still fragile.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`bench = yf.Ticker("^NSEI").history(period="1y")`<span class=""> fetches full 1-year history every time. </span><strong><span class="">No caching across process invocations.</span></strong><span class=""> Each screener run makes 1 live API call to yfinance. Not terrible but wasteful.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Actually wait - </span>`_benchmark_cache`<span class=""> is a module-level global. Within a single process it caches. But in the pipeline, ml_features is called for every candidate, all within the same run_live_preview process. So the first call fetches, subsequent calls reuse. Good.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`market_regime = 1 if Close &gt;= SMA50 else 0`<span class=""> uses </span>`bench['Close'].rolling(50).mean()`<span class=""> with default min_periods=50. So the first 49 days are NaN. Then filled with 1.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">The </span>`df['market_regime']`<span class=""> alignment: it uses </span>`reindex(df_dates).ffill()`<span class="">. If df_dates extends beyond bench_dates (unlikely) it would be NaN, filled with 1. Fine.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`df['sharpe_rank'] = 1 / (1 + np.exp(-ann_sharpe))`<span class=""> - sigmoid of annualized Sharpe. Range (0,1). OK.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong>`train-serve skew`</strong><span class="">: In </span>`run_model_training.py`<span class="">, the code computes </span>`full_df['sharpe_rank'] = 1 / (1 + np.exp(-ann_sharpe))`<span class=""> - same. Good, aligned.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">But </span>`run_model_training.py`<span class=""> doesn't engineer </span>`rel_rsi`<span class="">, </span>`ema_dist`<span class="">, </span>`vol_cluster`<span class="">, </span>`market_regime`<span class=""> the same way as </span>`ml_features.py`<span class="">. Let me check...</span></p></li></ul><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">In </span>`run_model_training.py::extract_features`<span class="">: </span>`df['dist_high']`<span class="">, </span>`df['ema_dist']`<span class="">, </span>`df['vol_cluster']`<span class="">, </span>`df['rsi']`<span class="">, </span>`df['sharpe_raw']`<span class="">. Then </span>`df = df.join(benchmark_df)`<span class=""> where </span>`benchmark_df`<span class=""> has </span>`market_regime`<span class=""> and </span>`benchmark_rsi`<span class="">. Then </span>`df['rel_rsi'] = df['rsi'] - df['benchmark_rsi']`<span class="">.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">In </span>`ml_features.py::extract_quantitative_features`<span class="">: same features. </span><strong><span class="">BUT</span></strong><span class="">: in training, </span>`bench['regime'] = np.where(bench['Close'] &gt;= bench['Close'].rolling(50).mean(), 1, 0)`<span class=""> uses default min_periods=50. In </span>`ml_features.py`<span class="">, </span>`_get_benchmark_data`<span class=""> uses the same. OK aligned.</span></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Actually wait - in </span>`run_model_training.py`<span class="">, the benchmark is a </span><strong><span class="">synthetic equal-weight average of all EQ stocks</span></strong><span class=""> in the DB (</span>`raw_df.groupby('trade_date')['close_price'].mean()`<span class="">). In </span>`ml_features.py`<span class="">, the benchmark is </span><strong><span class="">actual NIFTY 50 from yfinance</span></strong><span class="">. </span><strong><span class="">These are different benchmarks.</span></strong><span class=""> So the model is trained with </span>`market_regime`<span class=""> and </span>`rel_rsi`<span class=""> computed against a synthetic index, but served with </span>`market_regime`<span class=""> and </span>`rel_rsi`<span class=""> computed against the actual NIFTY 50. </span><strong><span class="">This is a serious train-serve skew bug.</span></strong></p><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">This is a </span><strong><span class="">critical finding</span></strong><span class=""> the first audit missed.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong>`run_model_training.py`</strong><span class="">:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`X_train = train_df[FEATURE_COLS]`<span class=""> etc. Features: </span>`['sharpe_rank', 'dist_high', 'market_regime', 'rel_rsi', 'ema_dist', 'vol_cluster']`<span class="">.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`train_end_date = unique_dates[split_date_idx - 5]`<span class=""> - this is the "embargo" - it drops the 5 dates immediately before the split. But it drops </span><strong><span class="">rows</span></strong><span class="">, not dates in the alignment sense. Actually the indexing is </span>`clean_df[clean_df.index &lt; train_end_date]`<span class=""> so it drops dates between split_date_idx-5 and split_date_idx. That's a 5-day gap. Good.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">But the label uses </span>`shift(-5)`<span class="">, so the last 5 rows of train_df still have labels that extend into the test period. Actually since we drop the last 5 dates, and labels use forward 5-day returns, the last training row's label is 5 days ahead of it, which is within the embargo gap. So no leakage. Good.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">BUT</span></strong><span class="">: The 80/20 split is a single split. No cross-validation. F3 already addressed.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Also</span></strong><span class="">: The metadata writes </span>`"features": FEATURE_COLS`<span class="">. When </span>`ml_predictor`<span class=""> loads, it does </span>`expected_features = getattr(global_model, 'feature_names_in_', None)`<span class="">. XGBoost models don't have </span>`feature_names_in_`<span class=""> by default (that's sklearn). So </span>`expected_features`<span class=""> falls back to the hardcoded list. That hardcoded list happens to match, but if you ever retrain with different features, the model will silently predict on wrong features. </span><strong><span class="">Add explicit feature metadata to the pickle or use </span>`model.get_booster().feature_names`<span class="">.</span></strong></p></li></ul></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Features are ordered.</span></strong><span class=""> </span>`FEATURE_COLS = ['sharpe_rank', 'dist_high', 'market_regime', 'rel_rsi', 'ema_dist', 'vol_cluster']`<span class="">. But the feature values are extracted per row - are they in that order? Yes, </span>`train_df[FEATURE_COLS]`<span class=""> and </span>`latest_features = df.iloc[-1:][list(expected_features)]`<span class="">. Both use list-based ordering. OK.</span></p></li><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">AUC 0.53 gate</span></strong><span class="">: The PRD says AUC &gt; 0.53. But without proper CV, this is trivially achievable by overfitting. See F3.</span></p></li></ol><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">Layer 4: Multi-agent Debate</span></strong></p><ol start="17"><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><strong><span class="">debate_graph.py</span></strong><span class="">:</span></p><ul><li><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph">`deterministic_risk_node`<span class=""> calls </span>`calculate_deterministic_risk_and_position`<span class=""> with </span>`portfolio_capital_rupees=settings.ALGO_ALLOCATED_CAPITAL`<span class="">. But </span>`ALGO_ALLOCATED_CAPITAL`<span class=""> is a static config. If the user has realized losses/gains, the actual capital differs. The arbiter itself computes </span>`core_equity = portfolio_capital_rupees + realized_pnl`<span class="">. So actually the arbiter internally compensates. But then the </span>`MAX_SECTOR_ALLOCATION_PCT`<span class=""> and other checks use </span>`portfolio_capital_rupees`<span class=""> (the static 10L), not </span>`core_equity`<span class="">. </span><strong><span class="">Inconsistency</span></strong><span class="">: sector cap uses 10L, but per-trade risk uses equity. Should use </span>`core_equity`<span class=""> consistently.</span></p></li></ul><p class="ds-markdown-paragraph"><span class="">Wait let me re-read:</span></p><div class="md-code-block
